					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Head of the Catholic Church from 1740 to 1758</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">"Benedict XIV" redirects here. For the name used by two antipopes, see <a href="/wiki/Antipope_Benedict_XIV" title="Antipope Benedict XIV">Antipope Benedict XIV</a>.</div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">"Cardinal Lambertini" redirects here. Not to be confused with <a href="/wiki/Egano_Righi-Lambertini" title="Egano Righi-Lambertini">Egano Righi-Lambertini</a>.</div>
<p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>

<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: gold;"><span style="font-size:80%">Papal styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">Pope Benedict XIV</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Coat_of_arms_of_Pope_Benedict_XIV.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Coat_of_arms_of_Pope_Benedict_XIV.svg/60px-Coat_of_arms_of_Pope_Benedict_XIV.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="73" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Coat_of_arms_of_Pope_Benedict_XIV.svg/90px-Coat_of_arms_of_Pope_Benedict_XIV.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Coat_of_arms_of_Pope_Benedict_XIV.svg/120px-Coat_of_arms_of_Pope_Benedict_XIV.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="871" data-file-height="1062" /></a></span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/His_Holiness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Holiness">His Holiness</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Your Holiness</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religious style</th><td class="infobox-data">Holy Father</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Posthumous style</th><td class="infobox-data">None</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Pope Benedict XIV</b> (<a href="/wiki/Latin_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Latin language">Latin</a>: <i lang="la">Benedictus XIV</i>; <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Benedetto XIV</i>; 31 March 1675&#160;– 3 May 1758),<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> born <b>Prospero Lorenzo Lambertini</b>, was head of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> and ruler of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a> from 17 August 1740 to his death in May 1758.<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;note 1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Perhaps one of the best scholars to sit on the papal throne, yet often overlooked, he promoted scientific learning, the Baroque arts, reinvigoration of <a href="/wiki/Thomism" title="Thomism">Thomism</a>, and the study of the human form.  Firmly committed to carrying out the decrees of the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Trent" title="Council of Trent">Council of Trent</a> and authentic Catholic teaching, Benedict removed changes previously made to the <a href="/wiki/Breviary" title="Breviary">Breviary</a>, sought peacefully to reverse growing secularism in European courts, invigorated ceremonies with great pomp, and throughout his life and his reign published numerous theological and ecclesiastical treatises.  In governing the Papal States, he reduced taxation on some products, but also raised taxes on others; he also encouraged agriculture and supported free trade within the Papal States. A scholar, he created the Sacred and Profane Museums, now part of the present <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Museums" title="Vatican Museums">Vatican Museum</a>.  He can be considered a <a href="/wiki/Polymath" title="Polymath">polymath</a> to an extent due to his numerous studies of ancient literature, his publishing of ecclesiastical books and documents, his interest in the study of the <a href="/wiki/Human_body" title="Human body">human body</a>, and his devotion to art and theology.
</p><p>Towards the end of his papacy Benedict XIV was forced to contend with issues surrounding the <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Jesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of Jesus">Society of Jesus</a>. He expelled them from <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a> at the behest of <a href="/wiki/Joseph_I_of_Portugal" title="Joseph I of Portugal">Joseph I</a> in 1758, just before his death. The papacy reluctantly acceded to the anti-Jesuit demands while providing minimal theological justification for the suppressions.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Horace_Walpole" title="Horace Walpole">Horace Walpole</a> described him as "loved by papists, esteemed by Protestants, a priest without insolence or interest, a prince without favorites, a pope without nepotism, an author without vanity, a man whom neither intellect nor power could corrupt."<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Birth_and_studies">Birth and studies</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Birth and studies">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Lambertini was born into a noble family of <a href="/wiki/Bologna" title="Bologna">Bologna</a>, the third of five children of Marcello Lambertini and Lucrezia Bulgarini.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>  At the time of his birth, Bologna was the second largest city in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a>.  His earliest studies were with tutors, and then he was sent to the Convitto del Porto, staffed by the <a href="/wiki/Somaschi_Fathers" title="Somaschi Fathers">Somaschi Fathers</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>  At the age of thirteen, he began attending the <a href="/wiki/Collegio_Clementino" title="Collegio Clementino">Collegio Clementino</a> in Rome, where he studied rhetoric, Latin, philosophy, and theology (1689–1692).  During his studies as a young man, he often studied the works of <a href="/wiki/St._Thomas_Aquinas" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Thomas Aquinas">St. Thomas Aquinas</a>, who was his favorite author and saint.  While he enjoyed studying at Collegio Clementino, his attention turned toward civil and canon law.  Soon after, in 1694 at the age of nineteen, he received the degree of <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_Sacred_Theology" title="Doctor of Sacred Theology">Doctor of Sacred Theology</a> and <a href="/wiki/Doctor_Utriusque_Juris" class="mw-redirect" title="Doctor Utriusque Juris">Doctor Utriusque Juris</a> (both ecclesiastical and civil law).<sup id="cite_ref-Healy_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Healy-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ecclesiastical_career">Ecclesiastical career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Ecclesiastical career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Lambertini became an assistant to Msgr. Alessandro Caprara, the Auditor of the Rota.  After the election of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Clement_XI" title="Pope Clement XI">Pope Clement XI</a> in November 1700, he was made a <a href="/wiki/Consistorial_advocate" class="mw-redirect" title="Consistorial advocate">consistorial advocate</a> in 1701.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>  Shortly after, he was created a Consultor of the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Sacred_Congregation_of_the_Roman_and_Universal_Inquisition" class="mw-redirect" title="Supreme Sacred Congregation of the Roman and Universal Inquisition">Supreme Sacred Congregation of the Roman and Universal Inquisition</a>, and then in 1708 <a href="/wiki/Promoter_of_the_Faith" class="mw-redirect" title="Promoter of the Faith">Promoter of the Faith</a>.  As Promoter of the Faith, he achieved two major successes.  The first was the canonization of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_V" title="Pope Pius V">Pope Pius V</a>.  The second was the composition of his treatise on the process of the beatification and canonization of saints.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1712, Lambertini was named Canon Theologus of the Chapter of the Vatican Basilica and member of the <a href="/wiki/Sacred_Congregation_of_Rites" title="Sacred Congregation of Rites">Sacred Congregation of Rites</a>; in 1713 he was named <a href="/wiki/Monsignor" title="Monsignor">monsignor</a>; and in 1718 secretary of the <a href="/wiki/Sacred_Congregation_of_the_Council" class="mw-redirect" title="Sacred Congregation of the Council">Sacred Congregation of the Council</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 12 June 1724, only two weeks after his election, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XIII" title="Pope Benedict XIII">Pope Benedict XIII</a> appointed Lambertini <a href="/wiki/Titular_bishop" title="Titular bishop">titular bishop</a> of Theodosia.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Lambertini was consecrated a bishop in Rome, in the Pauline Chapel of the Vatican Palace, on 16 July 1724, by Pope Benedict XIII.  The co-consecrators were Giovanni Francesco Nicolai, titular Archbishop of Myra (Vicar of the Vatican Basilica), and Nicola Maria Lercari, titular Archbishop of Nazianzus (Papal Maestro di Camera).<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> In 1725, he served as the Canonist at the Roman Synod of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XIII" title="Pope Benedict XIII">Pope Benedict XIII</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1718, the <i>Istituto delle scienze ed Arti Liberali</i> in Bologna had begun construction of a chapel for everyday convenience dedicated to the Annunication of the Virgin Mary.  In 1725, Bishop Prospero Lambertini of Theodosia, who was working in the Roman Curia but was mindful of his origins, ordered the chapel to be painted. He handed over the work to Carlo Salarolo, who had the walls of the chapel adorned. Lambertini also ordered and paid for the painting above the main altar, an image of the Virgin being greeted by the angel, the work of <a href="/wiki/Marcantonio_Franceschini" title="Marcantonio Franceschini">Marcantonio Franceschini</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He was made Bishop of <a href="/wiki/Ancona" title="Ancona">Ancona</a> on 27 January 1727, and was permitted to retain the title of Archbishop, as well as all the offices which he had already been granted. He was also allowed to continue as Abbot Commendatory of the <a href="/wiki/Camaldolese" title="Camaldolese">Camaldolese</a> monastery of S. Stefano di Cintorio (Cemeterio) in the diocese of Pisa.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> In 1731, the new bishop had the main altar and the choir of the cathedral restored and renovated. Once he became pope, Lambertini remembered his former diocese, sending an annual gift to the Church of Ancona, of sacred vessels of gold or silver, altar appointments, vestments, and other items.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Cardinal">Cardinal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Cardinal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Bishop Lambertini was created a Cardinal on 9 December 1726, though the public announcement of his promotion was postponed until 30 April 1728.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> He was assigned the <a href="/wiki/Titular_church" title="Titular church">titular church</a> of <i><a href="/wiki/Santa_Croce_in_Gerusalemme" title="Santa Croce in Gerusalemme">Santa Croce in Gerusalemme</a></i> on 10 May 1728.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> He participated in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave,_1730" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal conclave, 1730">1730 conclave</a>.
</p><p>On 30 April 1731, Cardinal Lambertini was appointed <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Bologna" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Bologna">Archbishop of Bologna</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Clement_XII" title="Pope Clement XII">Pope Clement XII</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> During his time as archbishop, he composed an extensive treatise in three volumes, <i>De synodo dioecesana</i>, on the subject of the diocesan synod, presenting a synthesis of the history, Canon Law, practices, and procedures for the holding of those important meetings of the clergy of each diocese.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> He was in fact preparing the ground for the holding of a synod of his own for the diocese of Bologna, an expectation he first announced in a <i>Notificazione</i> of 14 October 1732. When the first edition of the <i>De Synodo</i> was published in 1748, however, the synod still had not taken place.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> He continued in the office of Archbishop of Bologna even after he became Pope, not finally resigning until 14 January 1754.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Election_to_the_papacy">Election to the papacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Election to the papacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Prospero_Lambertini_by_Giuseppe_Maria_Crespi.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Prospero_Lambertini_by_Giuseppe_Maria_Crespi.jpg/220px-Prospero_Lambertini_by_Giuseppe_Maria_Crespi.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="310" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Prospero_Lambertini_by_Giuseppe_Maria_Crespi.jpg/330px-Prospero_Lambertini_by_Giuseppe_Maria_Crespi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Prospero_Lambertini_by_Giuseppe_Maria_Crespi.jpg/440px-Prospero_Lambertini_by_Giuseppe_Maria_Crespi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="710" data-file-height="1000" /></a><figcaption>Cardinal Lambertini c. 1740.</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave,_1740" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal conclave, 1740">Papal conclave, 1740</a></div>
<p>After the death of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Clement_XII" title="Pope Clement XII">Pope Clement XII</a> on 6 February 1740, Cardinal Lambertini attended the <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave,_1740" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal conclave, 1740">papal conclave</a> to choose a successor. The Conclave opened on 18 February, but Lambertini did not arrive until 5 March. He was not one of the 'papabili', not being one of the favorites of any of the factions (Imperialists, Spanish, French, <a href="/wiki/Zelanti" title="Zelanti">Zelanti</a>). The Conclave lasted for six months.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> At first Cardinal Ottoboni, the Dean of the Sacred College, was favored to be elected, but a number of cardinals were opposed to him because he was the protector of France in the Papal Curia.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> His death on 29 February 1740 eliminated him from consideration.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cardinal Domenico Riviera of Urbino received a respectable number of votes for a while, and then, in July, Cardinal Pompeio Aldrovandi of Bologna. He had enemies, however, who assembled enough votes to ensure that he would never get the two-thirds needed to be elected. His greatest enemy, the Camerlengo Cardinal Annibale Albani, chose instead to support Cardinal Giacomo de Lanfredini of Florence, who worked in Rome in the Curia. In mid-August, Albani asked the leader of the Imperialist faction, Cardinal Niccolò del Giudice, to give a thought to Lambertini. After long deliberations, Lambertini was put forth to the cardinal electors as a compromise candidate, and it is reported that he said to the members of the College of Cardinals "If you wish to elect a saint, choose Gotti; a statesman, Aldrovandi; an honest man, me."<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Vincenzo_Ludovico_Gotti" title="Vincenzo Ludovico Gotti">Vincenzo Ludovico Gotti</a> (1664–1742) was professor of philosophy at the College of Saint Thomas, and perhaps the leading <a href="/wiki/Thomism" title="Thomism">Thomist</a> of his time.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Pompeo_Aldrovandi" title="Pompeo Aldrovandi">Aldrovandi</a> was a canon lawyer.
</p><p>This witticism appears to have assisted his cause, which also benefited from his reputation for deep learning, gentleness, wisdom, and conciliation in policy.<sup id="cite_ref-cicognani_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cicognani-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> On the evening of 17 August 1740 at around 9:00 pm, on the 255th ballot, he was elected pope and took the throne name of Benedict XIV in honour of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XIII" title="Pope Benedict XIII">Pope Benedict XIII</a> to whom he owed his ecclesiastical career.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> He was solemnly crowned by the <a href="/wiki/Protodeacon" title="Protodeacon">protodeacon</a> on 21 August 1740. By 30 August 1740 the famous ephemeral baroque structures of the Festival of the <a href="/wiki/Chinea" title="Chinea">Chinea</a> and the triumphal arch of Benedict XIV were erected by <a href="/wiki/Charles_III_of_Spain" title="Charles III of Spain">Charles III of Spain</a>, who was then <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Naples" title="Kingdom of Naples">King of Naples</a> and a papal vassal.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Pontificate">Pontificate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Pontificate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedict_XIV_by_Pietro_Bracci.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Benedict_XIV_by_Pietro_Bracci.JPG/220px-Benedict_XIV_by_Pietro_Bracci.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Benedict_XIV_by_Pietro_Bracci.JPG/330px-Benedict_XIV_by_Pietro_Bracci.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Benedict_XIV_by_Pietro_Bracci.JPG/440px-Benedict_XIV_by_Pietro_Bracci.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2736" data-file-height="3648" /></a><figcaption>Bust of Benedict XIV by <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Bracci" title="Pietro Bracci">Pietro Bracci</a>, <a href="/wiki/Museum_of_Grenoble" title="Museum of Grenoble">Museum of Grenoble</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Benedict XIV's papacy began in a time of great difficulties, fueled by anticlericalism and chiefly caused by the disputes between <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic</a> rulers and the <a href="/wiki/Papacy" class="mw-redirect" title="Papacy">papacy</a> about governmental demands to nominate <a href="/wiki/Bishop" title="Bishop">bishops</a> rather than leaving the appointment to the church. He managed to overcome most of these problems&#160;– the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a>'s disputes with the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Naples" title="Kingdom of Naples">Kingdom of Naples</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sardinia" title="Sardinia">Sardinia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a>, <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Venice" title="Republic of Venice">Venice</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Austria" title="Austria">Austria</a> were settled.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pastoralis_Romani_Pontificis"><i>Pastoralis Romani Pontificis</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Pastoralis Romani Pontificis">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The apostolic constitution <i>Pastoralis Romani Pontificis</i>,<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> which was Benedict's revision of the traditional <i>Coena Domini</i> <a href="/wiki/Anathematization" class="mw-redirect" title="Anathematization">anathematization</a>, was <a href="/wiki/Promulgation_(Catholic_canon_law)" title="Promulgation (Catholic canon law)">promulgated</a> on 30 March 1741. In it Benedict again excommunicated all members of Protestant sects, including Lutherans, Calvinists, <a href="/wiki/Huldrych_Zwingli" title="Huldrych Zwingli">Zwinglians</a>, and Huguenots. It ordered that ecumenical councils not be resorted to by opponents of papal decisions.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Its most stringent clause was §20:<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<blockquote><p>We excommunicate all those who shall by themselves or others, directly or indirectly, under whatever title or pretext, presume to invade, destroy, occupy and detain, wholly or in part, the City of Rome, the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Sicily" title="Kingdom of Sicily">Kingdom of Sicily</a>, the islands of Sardinia and Corsica, the territories on this side of Lesina, the patrimony of St. Peter in Tuscany, the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Spoleto" title="Duchy of Spoleto">Duchy of Spoleto</a>, the Counties of <a href="/wiki/Comtat_Venaissin" title="Comtat Venaissin">Venaissin</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sabina_(region)" title="Sabina (region)">Sabina</a>, the <a href="/wiki/March_of_Ancona" title="March of Ancona">March of Ancona</a>, Massa Trebaria, <a href="/wiki/Romagna" title="Romagna">Romagna</a>, Campagna, and the maritime provinces and their territories and places, and the territories under special commission of the Arnulfi, and our cities of Bologna, <a href="/wiki/Cesena" title="Cesena">Cesena</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rimini" title="Rimini">Rimini</a>, <a href="/wiki/Benevento" title="Benevento">Benevento</a>, <a href="/wiki/Perugia" title="Perugia">Perugia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Avignon" title="Avignon">Avignon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Citt%C3%A0_di_Castello" title="Città di Castello">Citta di Castello</a>, <a href="/wiki/Todi" title="Todi">Todi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ferrara" title="Ferrara">Ferrara</a>, <a href="/wiki/Comacchio" title="Comacchio">Comachio</a>, and other cities, territories and places, or rights, belonging to the Roman Church, and mediately or immediately subject to the said Roman Church; and likewise those who presume to usurp de facto, to disturb, to retain, or in various ways to trouble, the supreme jurisdiction, belonging in them to Us and to the said Roman Church; and likewise their adherents, patrons, and protectors, or those who aid, counsel or abet them in any way whatsoever.</p></blockquote>
<p>This clause, if applied, excommunicated the governments of Spain, France, and the Empire, in addition to lesser princes who held, without papal grant or investiture, territory claimed by the Papacy. The bull was smiled at even by Benedict himself, who once said, "I like to leave the Vatican lightnings asleep." Its application to the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Parma_and_Piacenza" title="Duchy of Parma and Piacenza">Duchy of Parma</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Clement_XIII" title="Pope Clement XIII">Pope Clement XIII</a> in 1768 had major consequences, including the beginning of expulsions of Jesuits from European states.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Finances">Finances</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Finances">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>At the beginning of his reign, the papal government was heavily in debt, to the amount of 56,000,000 scudi, and was running an annual deficit of more than 200,000 scudi. Benedict attempted to improve the finances of the Papal States, but even at his death the administration was still running a deficit.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> His greatest economic action was the reduction in the size of the papal army, which had become ineffectual in terms of contemporary military practice, even in keeping order inside the Papal States; and he severely reduced the pay of both officers and soldiers.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> He instituted economies in his own household and in the bureaucracy, but these were insignificant in terms of the debt and deficit. In 1741, on the advice of Cardinal Aldovrandini (who had nearly been elected pope instead of Benedict), he instituted a new tax, a duty on stamped paper on legal documents; it did not produce the revenue expected, and it was abolished in 1743. He reduced taxes on imported cattle, oil, and raw silk, but imposed new taxes on lime, china clay, salt, wine, straw, and hay. In 1744 he raised taxes on land, house rents, feudal grants to barons, and pensions derived from prebends.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite these fiscal problems, the Papacy was able to buy two frigates in England, and in April 1745 Benedict personally christened a galley, named the <i>Benedetta</i>, which he had ordered constructed. He also ordered the modernization of the harbor of <a href="/wiki/Anzio" title="Anzio">Anzio</a>, but the work was so expensive that it had to be abandoned in 1752.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He encouraged agriculture and free trade and drastically cut the military budget, but was unable to completely reform the administration, still corrupt from previous papacies. At the University of Bologna he revived the practice of anatomical studies and established a chair of surgery. He had a clear view of ecclesiastical problems, had respect for differing opinions and an ability to distinguish between dogma and theory.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_activities">Other activities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Other activities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Benoit_XIV.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Benoit_XIV.jpg/220px-Benoit_XIV.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="254" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Benoit_XIV.jpg/330px-Benoit_XIV.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Benoit_XIV.jpg/440px-Benoit_XIV.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1709" data-file-height="1975" /></a><figcaption>Benedict XIV in 1741, portrait by <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Subleyras" title="Pierre Subleyras">Subleyras</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>On 22 December 1741, Benedict XIV issued the Bull <i>Immensa Pastorum Principis</i> and sent an Apostolic Brief to the Bishops of <a href="/wiki/Colonial_Brazil" title="Colonial Brazil">Brazil</a> and King John of Portugal, against the enslavement of the indigenous peoples of the Americas and other countries. It excommunicated any person who, for whatever motive, enslaved a native Brazilian. It did not address the case of black Africans. The Bull ordered the Jesuits to cease engaging in commerce, which was strictly forbidden by their own statutes, and meddling in politics. The bull went unenforced in Brazil.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_constitution" title="Apostolic constitution">Apostolic constitution</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Sacramentum_Poenitentiae" title="Sacramentum Poenitentiae">Sacramentum Poenitentiae</a></i> of 1741<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> assigned to the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith">Supreme Sacred Congregation of the Roman and Universal Inquisition</a> the responsibility of safeguarding the sanctity of the <a href="/wiki/Sacrament_of_Penance" title="Sacrament of Penance">sacrament of penance</a>.
</p><p>On 18 May 1743, Benedict XIV signed a document addressed to the Archbishops and Bishops of the <a href="/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Lithuanian_Commonwealth" title="Polish–Lithuanian Commonwealth">Kingdom of Poland</a> regarding marriage,<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> communicating his dissatisfaction with the dissolution of Christian marriages, some even long-stable ones, by the <a href="/wiki/Ecclesiastical_court" title="Ecclesiastical court">Ecclesiastical Courts</a> of Poland without due cause or in violation of <a href="/wiki/Canon_law" title="Canon law">canon law</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Troubles arose from what are called "clandestine marriages", a secret arrangement between partners, usually for the purpose of marrying a person of choice rather than entering into an "arranged marriage".<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Benedict XIV was also responsible, along with <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Silvio_Passionei" title="Domenico Silvio Passionei">Cardinal Passionei</a>, for beginning the catalogue of the oriental manuscripts in the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Library" title="Vatican Library">Vatican Library</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The Pope added some 3,300 of his own books to the collection. In 1741 the collection of manuscripts relating to Chinese religion and history were left to the Vatican Library by bequest of Msgr. Fouchet, a one-time missionary.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> During his reign the library of Marchese Alessandro Capponi was acquired through bequest. The collection of the antiquarian <a href="/wiki/Philipp_von_Stosch" title="Philipp von Stosch">Filippo Stosch</a> of Florence also came to the Vatican Library after his death, including a large collection of manuscripts that went back as far as the twelfth century.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1747, Benedict promulgated the bull <i><a href="/wiki/Postremo_mense" title="Postremo mense">Postremo mense superioris anni</a></i>, which summarised and restated certain aspects of Catholic teaching on <a href="/wiki/Infant_baptism" title="Infant baptism">infant baptism</a>, in particular that 1) it is generally not licit to baptise a child of a Jewish family without parental consent, 2) it is licit to baptise a Jewish child in danger of death without parental consent, 3) once such a baptism had occurred (whether licit or not), the ecclesiastical authorities have a duty to remove the child from its parents' custody in order to provide it with a Christian education.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Oriental_rites">Oriental rites</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Oriental rites">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Since his days as a Consultor at the Holy Office (Inquisition), Benedict had been involved in issues pertaining to the missions, both those seeking to convert non-Christians, and those seeking to reconcile heretics and schismatics to the Roman Church.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> One concern was the Coptic Christians in upper Egypt, where efforts to seek union with the Coptic Patriarch had not been successful. Numbers of Coptic priests and laity had entered into union with Rome, but had no bishop to serve their needs. In the Bull <i>Quemadmodum ingenti</i> of 4 August 1741, Benedict entrusted their care to the one Coptic bishop who was in union with Rome, the Patriarch Athanasius of Jerusalem, who was given extensive powers to supervise uniate Copts in Egypt.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In his encyclical <i>Allatae Sunt</i>, promulgated on 26 July 1755, and sent to missionaries working under the direction of the Congregation <i>de propaganda fide</i>,<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Pope Benedict addressed the numerous problems arising in dealing with the clergy and laity belonging to various eastern rites, particularly the Armenian and Syriac rites. He reminded the missionaries that they were converting people from schism and heresy:<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<blockquote><p>We also wanted to make clear to all the good will which the Apostolic See feels for Oriental Catholics in commanding them to observe fully their ancient rites which are not at variance with the Catholic religion or with propriety. The Church does not require schismatics to abandon their rites when they return to Catholic unity, but only that they forswear and detest heresy. Its great desire is for the preservation, not the destruction of different peoples&#160;&#8211;&#32;in short, that all may be Catholic rather than all become Latin.</p></blockquote>
<p>Benedict XIV, however, echoing the words of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Gelasius_I" title="Pope Gelasius I">Pope Gelasius I</a>, universally banned the practice of females serving the priest at the altar, noting that the practice had spread to certain Oriental Rites.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Chinese_rites_and_Indian_rites">Chinese rites and Indian rites</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Chinese rites and Indian rites">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pompeo_Girolamo_Batoni_-_Pope_Benedict_XIV_(Minneapolis_Institute_of_Arts).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Pompeo_Girolamo_Batoni_-_Pope_Benedict_XIV_%28Minneapolis_Institute_of_Arts%29.jpg/250px-Pompeo_Girolamo_Batoni_-_Pope_Benedict_XIV_%28Minneapolis_Institute_of_Arts%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="179" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Pompeo_Girolamo_Batoni_-_Pope_Benedict_XIV_%28Minneapolis_Institute_of_Arts%29.jpg/375px-Pompeo_Girolamo_Batoni_-_Pope_Benedict_XIV_%28Minneapolis_Institute_of_Arts%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Pompeo_Girolamo_Batoni_-_Pope_Benedict_XIV_%28Minneapolis_Institute_of_Arts%29.jpg/500px-Pompeo_Girolamo_Batoni_-_Pope_Benedict_XIV_%28Minneapolis_Institute_of_Arts%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="2861" /></a><figcaption>Benedict XIV presents his encyclical <i>Ex Omnibus</i> to the Comte de Stainville <a href="/wiki/%C3%89tienne_Fran%C3%A7ois,_duc_de_Choiseul" class="mw-redirect" title="Étienne François, duc de Choiseul">Étienne François</a>, later the Duc de Choiseul.</figcaption></figure>
<p>He had a very active papacy, reforming the education of priests, the <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">calendar of feasts</a> of the church, and many papal institutions. Perhaps the most important act of Benedict XIV's pontificate was the <a href="/wiki/Promulgation_(Catholic_canon_law)" title="Promulgation (Catholic canon law)">promulgation</a> of his famous laws about <a href="/wiki/Mission_(Christian)" class="mw-redirect" title="Mission (Christian)">missions</a> in the two <a href="/wiki/Papal_bull" title="Papal bull">bulls</a>, <i>Ex quo singulari</i> (11 July 1742),<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> and <i>Omnium sollicitudinum</i> (12 September 1744).<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> In these bulls he ruled on the custom of accommodating non-Christian words and usages to express Christian ideas and practices of the native cultures, which had been extensively done by the <a href="/wiki/Jesuits" title="Jesuits">Jesuits</a> in their <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">Indian</a> and <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">Chinese</a> missions. An example of this is the statues of ancestors&#160;– there had long been uncertainty whether honour paid to one's ancestors was unacceptable '<a href="/wiki/Ancestor_worship" class="mw-redirect" title="Ancestor worship">ancestor worship</a>,' or if it was something more like the Catholic <a href="/wiki/Veneration_of_saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Veneration of saints">veneration of the saints</a>. This question was especially pressing in the case of an ancestor known not to have been a Christian. The choice of a Chinese translation for the <a href="/wiki/Names_of_God" title="Names of God">name of God</a> had also been debated since the early 17th century. Benedict XIV denounced these practices in these two bulls. The consequence of this was that many of these converts left the church.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During his papacy, Benedict XIV commissioned a team of architects, led by <a href="/wiki/Nicola_Salvi" title="Nicola Salvi">Nicola Salvi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Vanvitelli" title="Luigi Vanvitelli">Luigi Vanvitelli</a>, to design a large palace that was to be 'more complex and with greater baroque style than the box of a palace Vanvitelli designed in <a href="/wiki/Caserta" title="Caserta">Caserta</a>'.  The palace was to be built south of St. Peter's Basilica, but was never built, as the plans were quietly ignored by Benedict's successor, <a href="/wiki/Clement_XIII" class="mw-redirect" title="Clement XIII">Clement XIII</a>.  They were brought up once more by <a href="/wiki/Pius_VI" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius VI">Pius VI</a> late in his papacy, but had to stop due to the possibility of invasion.  On 15 December 1744, Benedict XIV blessed the baroque chapel (Chapel of St. John the Baptist) in <a href="/wiki/Sant%27Antonio_dei_Portoghesi" title="Sant&#39;Antonio dei Portoghesi">Sant'Antonio dei Portoghesi</a> in Rome, which featured mosaics on the sides, floor, and wall behind the altar made of semi-precious stones.  The chapel, which had been commissioned by King John V of Portugal in 1740, was designed by Nicola Salvi and Luigi Vanvitelli.  When complete, it was then shipped to Portugal to be placed in the Igreja de Sāo Roque, the Jesuit church in Lisbon.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Friars_Minor_Capuchin" title="Order of Friars Minor Capuchin">Capuchins</a>, under the leadership of Italian priest Joseph Mary Bernini, grew the community of <a href="/wiki/Bettiah_Christians" title="Bettiah Christians">Bettiah Christians</a> in colonial India.<sup id="cite_ref-UCA2020_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UCA2020-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> He was invited to spread Catholic Christianity by Maharaja Dhurup Singh of the <a href="/wiki/Bettiah_Raj" title="Bettiah Raj">Bettiah Raj</a>, an appointment that was blessed by Pope Benedict XIV on 1 May 1742 in a personal letter to the king.<sup id="cite_ref-UCA2020_59-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UCA2020-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Consistories">Consistories</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Consistories">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_Benedict_XIV" title="Cardinals created by Benedict XIV">Cardinals created by Benedict XIV</a></div>
<p>Benedict XIV created 64 cardinals in seven consistories; among the new cardinals he elevated into the cardinalate was the <a href="/wiki/Henry_Benedict_Stuart" title="Henry Benedict Stuart">Henry Benedict Stuart</a> (1747). The pope also reserved one cardinal <i><a href="/wiki/In_pectore" title="In pectore">in pectore</a></i> and revealed that name at a later time, therefore validating the creation.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonizations_and_beatifications">Canonizations and beatifications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Canonizations and beatifications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The pope canonized seven saints during his pontificate including <a href="/wiki/Camillus_de_Lellis" title="Camillus de Lellis">Camillus de Lellis</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fidelis_of_Sigmaringen" title="Fidelis of Sigmaringen">Fidelis of Sigmaringen</a>. He also beatified several individuals such as <a href="/wiki/Charlemagne" title="Charlemagne">Charlemagne</a> and <a href="/wiki/Niccol%C3%B2_Albergati" title="Niccolò Albergati">Niccolò Albergati</a>.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Jubilee">Jubilee</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Jubilee">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>On 5 May 1749, Pope Benedict XIV declared a <a href="/wiki/Holy_Year" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Year">Holy Year</a>, to begin on Christmas Eve, 1749 and to extend throughout the next year until Christmas 1750.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> During the month of April 1750, 43,000 meals were served to the poor at the Trinita Hospital.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> Later that year, the Pope banned <a href="/wiki/Card_game" title="Card game">card games</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Gazette_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gazette-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With the <a href="/wiki/Papal_bull" title="Papal bull">papal bull</a> <i>Peregrinantes</i>, Benedict XIV convoked a <a href="/wiki/Jubilee_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Jubilee in the Catholic Church">Jubilee</a> in 1750. Furthermore, the pope called upon <a href="/wiki/Leonard_of_Port_Maurice" title="Leonard of Port Maurice">Saint Leonard of Port Maurice</a> to preach; both had a close relationship and the year previously saw Benedict XIV ask him to give sermons on penance and conversion in Rome. Among the initiatives that the pope designed for the Jubilee were the call for Christian unity and organizing for proper accommodation for those pilgrims who flocked to Rome.
</p><p>Upon the advice of Saint Leonard, the pope was the first to institute the <i><a href="/wiki/Via_Crucis" class="mw-redirect" title="Via Crucis">Via Crucis</a></i> at the <a href="/wiki/Colosseum" title="Colosseum">Colosseum</a>, which he consecrated as a site of martyrdom for the early Christians. The pope placed the stations of the Cross in the arena, however, these were removed in 1874 before being restored in 1925.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Personality">Personality</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Personality">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Benedict XIV was considered one of the best scholars to have sat on the papal throne, noted for his keen intellect and his consistent morals. Noted for his modesty, <a href="/wiki/Horace_Walpole" title="Horace Walpole">Horace Walpole</a> procured for the pope a statue of him with an accompanying plaque. When the copy of the inscription was forwarded to Rome for approval, Benedict XIV smiled upon reading it and, shaking his head, exclaimed: "Alas! I am like the statues of the Piazza San Pietro – admirable at a distance but monstrous when seen at close quarters!"<sup id="cite_ref-Pickle_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pickle-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>However, Benedict XIV also had his personal faults, unable to resist gambling in cards and was known for the use of profane language which one of his early biographers classified as "unfortunate phraseology". Despite his best efforts, the pontiff was unable to completely cut out bad language. A deeply pious man, Benedict XIV had a crucifix placed in every room, and it is said that the sight of a crucifix helped to restrain his poor language more often than not.<sup id="cite_ref-Pickle_63-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pickle-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite his faults, Benedict XIV was jovial and was lucid until the very end. To those who knew him, his blue eyes seemed to sparkle with humor and cordiality.<sup id="cite_ref-Pickle_63-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pickle-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death_and_burial">Death and burial</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Death and burial">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:BentoXIVb.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/BentoXIVb.jpg/220px-BentoXIVb.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="183" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/BentoXIVb.jpg/330px-BentoXIVb.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/BentoXIVb.jpg/440px-BentoXIVb.jpg 2x" data-file-width="737" data-file-height="612" /></a><figcaption>Tomb of Benedict XIV, <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">St. Peter's Basilica</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Benedict XIV had suffered from kidney problems for years. His health worsened in 1758 and, after a battle with <a href="/wiki/Gout" title="Gout">gout</a>, he died on 3 May 1758 at the age of 83.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> His final words to those surrounding him on his deathbed were, "I leave you in the hands of God."<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Despite those words, the pope is alleged to have recited the famous Latin phrase "<a href="/wiki/Sic_transit_gloria_mundi" title="Sic transit gloria mundi">Sic transit gloria mundi</a>" before poignantly adding "I now fall into silence and forgetfulness, the only place that belongs to me".
</p><p>Benedict XIV originally demonstrated symptoms of illness on 26 April 1758 after having previously rallied from a serious illness that included gout that February. However, a contracted <a href="/wiki/Fever" title="Fever">fever</a> aggravated his <a href="/wiki/Asthma" title="Asthma">asthma</a> and he suffered from difficulties in urinating. However, his condition deteriorated rapidly in the following days after his gout and contracted <a href="/wiki/Kidney_disease" title="Kidney disease">kidney disease</a> increased his sufferings. Despite this, his appetite had not diminished which was interpreted as a sign that he could potentially rally from his malady. However, into May, his condition had worsened considerably, though the pope remained lucid until the end, able to impart one last blessing before he died on 3 May.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following his funeral, he was interred in <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Basilica">Saint Peter's Basilica</a> and a large <a href="/wiki/Catafalque" title="Catafalque">catafalque</a> was erected in his honour.
</p>
<div style="clear:left;"></div>

<li><a href="/wiki/Castrato#Decline" title="Castrato">Castrato</a>&#160;– Benedict XIV was one of the first popes to voice displeasure regarding the use of castrated males in church choir services. He did nothing, however, to stop the practice.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/List_of_popes" title="List of popes">List of popes</a></li>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-3"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-3">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Antipope_Benedict_X" title="Antipope Benedict X">Pope Benedict X</a> (1058–1059) is now considered an <a href="/wiki/Antipope" title="Antipope">antipope</a>.  At the time, however, this status was not recognized by church historians, and so the tenth legitimate pontiff by this name is the one who took the official name <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XI" title="Pope Benedict XI">Benedict XI</a> (1303–1304).  This has advanced the numbering of all subsequent Popes Benedict by one.  Popes Benedict XI–XVI are therefore the tenth through fifteenth popes by that name.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XIV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

</li>

<li id="cite_note-41"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-41">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFBenedict_XIV1777" class="citation book cs1 cs1-prop-long-vol cs1-prop-foreign-lang-source">Benedict XIV (1777). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=U-0XktFfDwYC"><i>Bullarium Sanctissimi Domini Nostri Benedicti Pape Benedicti XIV Bullarium</i></a> (in Latin). XX.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Bullarium+Sanctissimi+Domini+Nostri+Benedicti+Pape+Benedicti+XIV+Bullarium&amp;rft.place=Venice&amp;rft.pages=65-68%2C+no.+XX&amp;rft.edition=recentior%2C+auctior%2C+et+emendatoir&amp;rft.pub=Occhi&amp;rft.date=1777&amp;rft.au=Benedict+XIV&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3DU-0XktFfDwYC&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Benedict+XIV" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<li id="cite_note-42"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-42">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.papalencyclicals.net/Ben14/b14nimia.htm">Benedict, XIV, "Nimiam Licentiam: To Bishops of Poland: On Validity of Marriages", May 18, 1743</a> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFBenedict_XIV1777" class="citation book cs1 cs1-prop-long-vol cs1-prop-foreign-lang-source">Benedict XIV (1777). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=U-0XktFfDwYC"><i>Bullarium Sanctissimi Domini Nostri Benedicti Pape Benedicti XIV</i></a> (in Latin). Vol.&#160;Tomus primus. Venice: Occhi. pp.&#160;301–306.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Bullarium+Sanctissimi+Domini+Nostri+Benedicti+Pape+Benedicti+XIV&amp;rft.place=Venice&amp;rft.pages=301-306&amp;rft.pub=Occhi&amp;rft.date=1777&amp;rft.au=Benedict+XIV&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3DU-0XktFfDwYC&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Benedict+XIV" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-43"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-43">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Benedict wrote, "At times it is argued that the marriage was entered upon by force or by fear, in either case without the free consent of one or the other of the contracting parties; at other times a legitimate and canonical impediment is alleged, which could have been known before the marriage was contracted if it had not been purposefully concealed; also at times, and this happens more frequently, a marriage is annulled because it was contracted before another priest, even with the consent of the parish priest or of the ordinary bishop but without the necessary and usual formalities. Certainly it is clear that these dissolutions of marriages in Poland are a source of evil and an open door to crime."</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-44"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-44">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Benedict wrote, "We had learned that an evil custom of hidden marriages, more popularly known as marriages of joint knowledge, has spread throughout much of the Christian world. Among the resulting irregularities is that hidden marriages of this sort were themselves being dissolved where other marriages were publicly celebrated." <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFMichael_Joseph_Schuck1991" class="citation book cs1">Michael Joseph Schuck (1991). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=lCy1YfERcNcC&amp;pg=PA33"><i>That They be One: The Social Teaching of the Papal Encyclicals, 1740-1989</i></a>. Washington DC: Georgetown University Press. p.&#160;7. <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-0-87840-489-6" title="Special:BookSources/978-0-87840-489-6"><bdi>978-0-87840-489-6</bdi></a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=That+They+be+One%3A+The+Social+Teaching+of+the+Papal+Encyclicals%2C+1740-1989&amp;rft.place=Washington+DC&amp;rft.pages=7&amp;rft.pub=Georgetown+University+Press&amp;rft.date=1991&amp;rft.isbn=978-0-87840-489-6&amp;rft.au=Michael+Joseph+Schuck&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3DlCy1YfERcNcC%26pg%3DPA33&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Benedict+XIV" class="Z3988"></span></span>
